--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: What is the standard actuarial discount rate used for calculating accelerated death benefit advances in life insurance?
reasoning: The query asks for a specific financial or actuarial metric — the "actuarial discount rate" applied to "accelerated death benefit advances." This is a highly technical term used in life insurance and actuarial science, referring to the rate used to discount future death benefits when they are paid early (accelerated) to policyholders with terminal illnesses.

The key challenge here is that this rate is not a universally fixed value; it is typically set by insurance companies or regulatory bodies based on specific assumptions (e.g., interest rates, mortality tables). However, the query does not specify a particular insurer, policy type, or jurisdiction, so it's asking for a general definition or standard practice.

Given that the term "actuarial discount rate" for accelerated death benefits is a well-defined concept in actuarial literature, the most direct way to answer this is to retrieve the standard definition or commonly accepted value used in industry practice (e.g., from NAIC guidelines, actuarial standards, or insurance policy documentation).

This is a single-fact query: it seeks a definition or standard value for a specific actuarial term. No chaining of facts is required — the answer can be found directly by identifying the standard rate or definition associated with this term.

Therefore, only one sub-goal is needed.
